Did you mean to convert \"\n            f\"to a Quantity with unit {m} using '<<'?\",\n            AstropyWarning,\n        )\n        return NotImplemented\n\n    def __hash__(self) -> int:\n        return self._hash\n\n    @cached_property\n    def _hash(self) -> int:\n        return hash((self.scale, *[x.name for x in self.bases], *map(str, self.powers)))\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n    def __eq__(self, other):\n        if self is other:\n            return True\n\n        try:\n            other = Unit(other, parse_strict=\"silent\")\n        except (ValueError, UnitsError, TypeError):\n            return NotImplemented\n\n        # Other is unit-like, but the test below requires it is a UnitBase\n        # instance; if it is not, give up (so that other can try).\n        if not isinstance(other, UnitBase):\n            return NotImplemented\n\n        try:\n            return is_effectively_unity(self._to(other))\n        except UnitsError:\n            return False\n\n    def __ne__(self, other):\n        return not (self == other)\n\n    def __le__(self, other):\n        scale = self._to(Unit(other))\n        return scale <= 1.0 or is_effectively_unity(scale)\n\n    def __ge__(self, other):\n        scale = self._to(Unit(other))\n        return scale >= 1.0 or is_effectively_unity(scale)\n\n    def __lt__(self, other):\n        return not (self >= other)\n\n    def __gt__(self, other):\n        return not (self <= other)\n\n    def __neg__(self) -> \"astropy.units.Quantity\":\n        return self * -1.0\n\n    def is_equivalent(self, other, equivalencies=[]):\n        \"\"\"Check whether this unit is equivalent to ``other``.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        other : `~astropy.units.Unit`, str, or tuple\n            The unit to convert to. If a tuple of units is specified, this\n            method returns true if the unit matches any of those in the tuple.\n\n        equivalencies : list of tuple\n            A list of equivalence pairs to try if the units are not\n            directly convertible.  See :ref:`astropy:unit_equivalencies`.\n            This list is in addition to possible global defaults set by, e.g.,\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        equivalencies : list of tuple\n            List of equivalent pairs, e.g., as returned by\n            `~astropy.units.dimensionless_angles`.\n        \"\"\"\n        self._reset_equivalencies()\n        return self.add_enabled_equivalencies(equivalencies)\n\n    def add_enabled_equivalencies(self, equivalencies):\n        \"\"\"\n        Adds to the set of equivalencies enabled in the unit registry.\n\n        These equivalencies are used if no explicit equivalencies are given,\n        both in unit conversion and in finding equivalent units.\n\n        This is meant in particular for allowing angles to be dimensionless.\n        Use with care.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        equivalencies : list of tuple\n            List of equivalent pairs, e.g., as returned by\n            `~astropy.units.dimensionless_angles`.\n        \"\"\"\n        # pre-normalize list to help catch mistakes\n        equivalencies = _normalize_equivalencies(equivalencies)\n        self._equivalencies |= set(equivalencies)\n\n    @property\n    def aliases(self) -> dict[str, UnitBase]:\n        return self._aliases\n\n    def set_enabled_aliases(self, aliases: dict[str, UnitBase]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Set aliases for units.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        aliases : dict of str, Unit\n            The aliases to set. The keys must be the string aliases, and values\n            must be the `astropy.units.Unit` that the alias will be mapped to.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        ValueError\n            If the alias already defines a different unit.\n\n        \"\"\"\n        self._reset_aliases()\n        self.add_enabled_aliases(aliases)\n\n    def add_enabled_aliases(self, aliases: dict[str, UnitBase]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Add aliases for units.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        aliases : dict of str, Unit\n            The aliases to add. The keys must be the string aliases, and values\n            must be the `astropy.units.Unit` that the alias will be mapped to.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        ValueError\n            If the alias already defines a different unit.\n\n        \"\"\"\n        for alias, unit in aliases.items():\n            if alias in self._registry and unit != self._registry[alias]:\n                raise ValueError(\n                    f\"{alias} already means {self._registry[alias]}, so \"\n                    f\"cannot be used as an alias for {unit}.\"\n                )\n            if alias in self._aliases and unit != self._aliases[alias]:\n                raise ValueError(\n                    f\"{alias} already is an alias for {self._aliases[alias]}, so \"\n                    f\"cannot be used as an alias for {unit}.\"\n                )\n\n        for alias, unit in aliases.items():\n            if alias not in self._registry and alias not in self._aliases:\n                self._aliases[alias] = unit\n\n\nclass _UnitContext:\n    def __init__(self, init=[], equivalencies=[]):\n        _unit_registries.append(_UnitRegistry(init=init, equivalencies=equivalencies))\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n_unit_registries: Final = [_UnitRegistry()]\n\n\ndef get_current_unit_registry() -> _UnitRegistry:\n    return _unit_registries[-1]\n\n\ndef set_enabled_units(units: object) -> _UnitContext:\n    \"\"\"\n    Sets the units enabled in the unit registry.\n\n    These units are searched when using\n    `UnitBase.find_equivalent_units`, for example.\n\n    This may be used either permanently, or as a context manager using\n    the ``with`` statement (see example below).\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    units : list of sequence, dict, or module\n        This is a list of things in which units may be found\n        (sequences, dicts or modules), or units themselves.  The\n        entire set will be \"enabled\" for searching through by methods\n        like `UnitBase.find_equivalent_units` and `UnitBase.compose`.\n\n    Examples\n    --------\n    >>> from astropy import units as u\n    >>> with u.set_enabled_units([u.pc]):\n    ...     u.m.find_equivalent_units()\n    ...\n      Primary name | Unit definition | Aliases\n    [\n      pc           | 3.08568e+16 m   | parsec  ,\n    ]\n    >>> u.m.find_equivalent_units()\n      Primary name | Unit definition | Aliases\n    [\n      AU           | 1.49598e+11 m   | au, astronomical_unit            ,\n      Angstrom     | 1e-10 m         | AA, angstrom, Å                  ,\n      cm           | 0.01 m          | centimeter                       ,\n      earthRad     | 6.3781e+06 m    | R_earth, Rearth                  ,\n      jupiterRad   | 7.1492e+07 m    | R_jup, Rjup, R_jupiter, Rjupiter ,\n      lsec         | 2.99792e+08 m   | lightsecond                      ,\n      lyr          | 9.46073e+15 m   | lightyear                        ,\n      m            | irreducible     | meter                            ,\n      micron       | 1e-06 m         |                                  ,\n      pc           | 3.08568e+16 m   | parsec                           ,\n      solRad       | 6.957e+08 m     | R_sun, Rsun                      ,\n    ]\n    \"\"\"\n    # and modified by Neil Crighton (neilcrighton@gmail.com), Roban Kramer\n# (robanhk@gmail.com), and Nathaniel Starkman (n.starkman@mail.utoronto.ca).\n\n# Many of these adapted from Hogg 1999, class' signature.\n\n    This provides around a 20x speedup for future calls of ``inspect.signature(cls)``.\n    `Cosmology` has a lot of I/O methods that use the signature, so this is a\n inspect.signature(cls)  # add the new signature to the class\n    return cls\n\n\ndef dataclass_decorator(cls: type[\"Cosmology\"], /) -> type[\"Cosmology\"]:\n    \"\"\"Decorator for the dataclass A `str` is assumed to be the correct format to use when\n            converting.\n            ``format`` is broadcast to match the shape of ``other``.\n            Note that the cosmology arguments are not broadcast against\n            ``format``, so it cannot determine the output shape.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        bool\n            True if cosmologies are equivalent, False otherwise.\n\n        Examples\n        --------\n        Two cosmologies may be equivalent even if not of the same class.\n        In this examples the ``LambdaCDM`` has ``Ode0`` set to the same value\n        calculated in ``FlatLambdaCDM``.\n\n            >>> import astropy.units as u\n            >>> from astropy.cosmology import LambdaCDM, FlatLambdaCDM\n            >>> cosmo1 = LambdaCDM(70 * (u.km/u.s/u.Mpc), 0.3, 0.7)\n            >>> cosmo2 = FlatLambdaCDM(70 * (u.km/u.s/u.Mpc), 0.3)\n            >>> cosmo1.is_equivalent(cosmo2)\n            True\n\n        While in this example, the cosmologies are not equivalent.\n\n            >>> cosmo3 = FlatLambdaCDM(70 * (u.km/u.s/u.Mpc), 0.3, Tcmb0=3 * u.K)\n            >>> cosmo3.is_equivalent(cosmo2)\n            False\n\n        Also, using the keyword argument, the notion of equivalence is extended\n        to any Python object that can be converted to a |Cosmology|.\n\n            >>> from astropy.cosmology import Planck18\n            >>> tbl = Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\")\n            >>> Planck18.is_equivalent(tbl, format=True)\n            True\n\n        The list of valid formats, e.g. the |Table| in this example, may be\n        checked with ``Cosmology.from_format.list_formats()``.\n\n        As can be seen in the list of formats, not all formats can be\n        auto-identified by ``Cosmology.from_format.registry``. This is similar to the error normally raised by Python\n            for an inconsistent method resolution order.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        type\n            A :class:`Cosmology` subclass this class inherits from that is not a\n            :class:`FlatCosmologyMixin` subclass.\n        \"\"\"\n        _kls = cls if kls is None else kls\n\n        # test_equivalency_context_manager():\n        assert x.decompose() == unit.decompose()\n\n    factored = x.compose(units=u.si)\n\n    for unit in factored:\n        assert x.decompose() == unit.decompose()\n\n\ndef test_compose_best_unit_first():\n    results = u.l.compose()\n    assert len(results[0].bases) == 1\n    assert results[0].bases[0] is u.l\n\n    results = (u.s**-1).compose()\n    assert results[0].bases[0] in (u.Hz, u.Bq)\n\n    results = (u.Ry.decompose()).compose()\n    assert results[0].bases[0] is u.Ry\n\n\ndef test_compose_no_duplicates():\n    new = u.kg / u.s**3 * u.au**2.5 / u.yr**0.5 / u.sr**2\n    composed = new.compose(units=u.cgs.bases)\n    assert len(composed) == 1\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"dtype\", tuple(map(\"\".join, itertools.product(\"<>\", \"if\", \"48\")))\n)\ndef test_endian_independence(dtype):\n    \"\"\"\n    Regression test for #744\n\n    A logic issue in the units code meant that big endian arrays could not be\n    converted because the dtype is '>f4', not 'float32', and the code was\n    looking for the strings 'float' or 'int'.\n    \"\"\"\n    x = np.array([1, 2, 3], dtype=dtype)\n    assert u.m.to(u.cm, x).tolist() == [100.0, 200.0, 300.0]\n\n\ndef test_radian_base():\n    \"\"\"\n    Issue #863\n    \"\"\"\n    assert (1 * u.degree).si.unit == u.rad\n\n\ndef test_no_as():\n    # We don't define 'as', since it is a keyword, but we\n    # do want to define the long form (`attosecond`).\n    assert not hasattr(u, \"as\")\n    assert hasattr(u, \"attosecond\")\n\n\ndef test_no_duplicates_in_names():\n    # Regression test for #5036\n    assert u.ct.names == [\"ct\", \"count\"]\n    assert u.ct.short_names == [\"ct\", \"count\"]\n    assert u.ct.long_names == [\"count\"]\n    assert set(u.ph.names) == set(u.ph.short_names) | set(u.ph.long_names)\n\n\ndef test_pickling():\n    p = pickle.dumps(u.m)\n    other = pickle.loads(p)\n\n    assert other is u.m\n\n    new_unit = u.IrreducibleUnit([\"foo\"], format={\"unicode\": \"bar\"})\n    # This is local, so the unit should not be registered.\n    assert \"foo\" not in u.get_current_unit_registry().registry\n\n    # Test pickling of this unregistered unit.\n    p = pickle.dumps(new_unit)\n    new_unit_copy = pickle.loads(p)\n    assert new_unit_copy is not new_unit\n    assert new_unit_copy.names == [\"foo\"]\n    assert new_unit_copy.to_string(\"unicode\") == \"bar\"\n    # It should still not be registered.\n    assert \"foo\" not in u.get_current_unit_registry().registry\n\n    # Now try the same with a registered unit.\n    with u.add_enabled_units([new_unit]):\n        p = pickle.dumps(new_unit)\n        assert \"foo\" in u.get_current_unit_registry().registry\n        new_unit_copy = pickle.loads(p)\n        assert new_unit_copy is new_unit\n\n    # Check that a registered unit can be loaded and that it gets re-enabled.\n    with u.add_enabled_units([]):\n        assert \"foo\" not in u.get_current_unit_registry().registry\n        new_unit_copy = pickle.loads(p)\n        assert new_unit_copy is not new_unit\n        assert new_unit_copy.names == [\"foo\"]\n        assert new_unit_copy.to_string(\"unicode\") == \"bar\"\n        assert \"foo\" in u.get_current_unit_registry().registry\n\n    # And test between sessions easily, so fake it.\n\n    This test can be changed if the pickle protocol or the code\n    changes enough that it no longer works.\n\n    \"\"\"\n    hash_m = hash(u.m)\n \"\n        b\"meter: base unit of length in SI\\x94ub.\"\n    )\n    assert unit is u.m\n    assert hash(u.m) == hash_m\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"unit\",\n problem with _hash being pickled\n    and restored only appeared if the unpickling was done in another\n    session, for which the hash no longer was valid, and it is difficult\n    to mimic separate sessions in a simple test. See gh-11872.\n    \"\"\"\n    unit_hash = hash(unit)\n    assert \"_hash\" in vars(unit)\n    unit_copy = pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(unit))\n    # unit is not registered so we get a copy.\n    assert unit_copy is not unit\n    assert \"_hash\" not in vars(unit_copy)\n    assert hash(unit_copy) == unit_hash\n    with u.add_enabled_units([unit]):\n        # unit is registered, so we get a reference.\n        unit_ref = pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(unit))\n        if isinstance(unit, u.IrreducibleUnit):\n            assert unit_ref is unit\n        else:\n            assert unit_ref is not unit\n        # pickle.load used to override the hash, although in this case\n        # it would be the same anyway, so not clear this tests much.\n        assert hash(unit) == unit_hash\n\n\ndef astropy.cosmology._src.tests.io.test_connect import (\n    ReadWriteTestMixin,\n    ToFromFormatTestMixin,\n)\nfrom astropy.table import Column, QTable, Table\n\nfrom .parameter.test_descriptors import (\n    ParametersAttributeTestMixin,\n)\nfrom .parameter.test_parameter import ParameterTestMixin\n\n##############################################################################\n# SETUP / TEARDOWN\n\n\ndef make_valid_zs(max_z: float = 1e5) -> tuple[list, NDArray[float], list, list]:\n    \"\"\"Make a list of valid redshifts for testing.\"\"\"\n    # scalar\n    scalar_zs = [\n        0,\n        1,\n        min(1100, max_z),  # interesting times\n        # FIXME! np.inf breaks some funcs. 0 * inf is an error\n        np.float64(min(3300, max_z)),  # different type\n        2 * cu.redshift,\n        3 * u.one,  # compatible units\n    ]\n    # array\n    _zarr = np.linspace(0, min(1e5, max_z), num=20)\n    array_zs = [\n        _zarr,  # numpy\n        _zarr.tolist(),  # pure python\n        Column(_zarr),  # table-like\n        _zarr * cu.redshift,  # Quantity\n    ]\n    return scalar_zs, _zarr, array_zs, scalar_zs + array_zs\n\n\nscalar_zs, z_arr, array_zs, valid_zs = make_valid_zs()\n\ninvalid_zs = [\n    (None, TypeError),  # wrong type\n    # Wrong units (the TypeError is for the cython, which can differ)\n    (4 * u.MeV, (u.UnitConversionError, TypeError)),  # scalar\n    ([0, 1] * u.m, (u.UnitConversionError, TypeError)),  # array\n]\n\n\n@dataclass_decorator\nclass SubCosmology(Cosmology):\n    \"\"\"Defined here to be serializable.\"\"\"\n\n    H0: Parameter = Parameter(unit=\"km/(s Mpc)\")\n    Tcmb0: Parameter = Parameter(default=0 * u.K, unit=u.K)\n    m_nu: Parameter = Parameter(default=0 * u.eV, unit=u.eV)\n\n test type\n        # value set at initialization\n        assert cosmo.meta == self.cls_kwargs.get(\"meta\", {})\n\n    def test_meta_mutable(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"The metadata is NOT immutable on a cosmology\"\"\"\n        key = next(iter(cosmo.meta.keys()))  # select some key\n        cosmo.meta[key] = cosmo.meta.pop(key)  # will error if immutable\n\n\nclass CosmologyTest(\n    ParameterTestMixin,\n    ParametersAttributeTestMixin,\n    MetaTestMixin,\n    ReadWriteTestMixin,\n    ToFromFormatTestMixin,\n    metaclass=abc.ABCMeta,\n):\n    \"\"\"Test subclasses of :class:`astropy.cosmology.Cosmology`.\"\"\"\n\n    @abc.abstractmethod\n    def setup_class(self):\n        \"\"\"Setup for testing.\"\"\"\n\n    def teardown_class(self):\n        pass\n\n    @property\n    def cls_args(self):\n        return tuple(self._cls_args.values())\n\n    @pytest.fixture(scope=\"class\")\n    def cosmo_cls(self):\n        \"\"\"The Cosmology class as a cosmology.\"\"\"\n        ba = inspect.signature(self.cls).bind(*self.cls_args, **self.cls_kwargs)\n        ba.apply_defaults()\n        return ba\n\n    @pytest.fixture(scope=\"class\")\n    def cosmo(self, cosmo_cls):\n        \"\"\"The cosmology instance with which to test.\"\"\"\n        ba = inspect.signature(self.cls).bind(*self.cls_args, **self.cls_kwargs)\n        ba.apply_defaults()\n ---------------------------------------------------------------\n    # class-level\n\n    def test_init_subclass(self, cosmo_cls):\n        \"\"\"Test creating subclasses registers classes and manages Parameters.\"\"\"\n\n        # -----------------------------------------------------------\n        # Normal subclass creation\n\n        class InitSubclassTest(cosmo_cls):\n            pass\n\n _COSMOLOGY_CLASSES.pop(InitSubclassTest.__qualname__)\n        assert registrant is InitSubclassTest\n\n        # -----------------------------------------------------------\n        # Skip\n\n        class UnRegisteredSubclassTest(cosmo_cls):\n            @classmethod\n            def _register_cls(cls):\n                \"\"\"Override to not register.\"\"\"\n\n        assert UnRegisteredSubclassTest.parameters == cosmo_cls.parameters\n        assert UnRegisteredSubclassTest.__qualname__ not in _COSMOLOGY_CLASSES\n\n    # cosmo.meta[\"m\"] == 1\n\n        # if meta is None, it is changed to a dict\n        cosmo = cosmo_cls(*self.cls_args, name=\"test_init\", meta=None)\n        assert cosmo.meta == {}\n\n    def test_name(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"Test property ``name``.\"\"\"\n        assert cosmo.name is None or isinstance(cosmo.name, str)  # type\n        assert cosmo.name == self.cls_kwargs[\"name\"]  # test has expected value\n\n    def test_name_immutable(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"The name field should be immutable.\"\"\"\n        match = \"cannot assign to field 'name'\"\n        with pytest.raises(AttributeError, match=match):\n            cosmo.name = None\n\n    def test_name_on_cls(self, cosmo_cls):\n        \"\"\"Test accessing :attr:`~astropy.cosmology.Cosmology.name` from the class.\"\"\"\n        assert cosmo_cls.name is None\n\n    @abc.abstractmethod\n    def test_is_flat(self, cosmo_cls, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"Test property ``is_flat``.\"\"\"\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------\n    # clone\n\n    def test_clone_identical(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"Test method ``.clone()`` if no (kw)args.\"\"\"\n        assert cosmo.clone() is cosmo\n\n    def test_clone_name(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"Test method ``.clone()`` name argument.\"\"\"\n        # test changing name. clone treats 'name' differently (see next test)\n        c = cosmo.clone(name=\"cloned cosmo\")\n        assert c.name == \"cloned cosmo\"  # changed\n        # show name is the only thing changed\n        object.__setattr__(c, \"name\", cosmo.name)  # first change name back\n        assert c == cosmo\n        assert c.meta == cosmo.meta\n\n        # now change a different parameter and see how 'name' changes\n        c = cosmo.clone(meta={\"test_clone_name\": True})\n        assert c.name == cosmo.name + \" (modified)\"\n\n    def test_clone_meta(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"Test method ``.clone()`` meta argument: cosmo.clone(meta=dict(test_clone_meta=True))\n        assert c.meta[\"test_clone_meta\"] is True\n        c.meta.pop(\"test_clone_meta\")  # remove from meta\n        assert c.meta == cosmo.meta  # \"\"\"\n\n    def test_clone_fail_unexpected_arg(self, cosmo):\n        \"\"\"Test when ``.clone()`` gets an unexpected argument.\"\"\"\n        with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=\"unexpected keyword argument\"):\n            cosmo.clone(not_an_arg=4)\n\n    def test_clone_fail_positional_arg(self, cosmo):\n        with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=\"1 positional argument\"):\n :meth:`astropy.cosmology.Cosmology.is_equivalent`.\"\"\"\n        # to self\n        assert cosmo.is_equivalent(cosmo)\n\n        # same class, different instance\n        newclone = # correct\n        assert cosmo == cosmo\n        # different name <= not equal, but equivalent\n        newcosmo = cosmo.clone(name=\"test_equality\")\n        assert (cosmo != newcosmo) and pytest.raises(AttributeError):\n                setattr(cosmo, n, getattr(cosmo, n))\n\n    def test_pickle_class(self, cosmo_cls, pickle_protocol):\n        \"\"\"Test classes can pickle and unpickle.\"\"\"\n        # pickle and unpickle\n        f = pickle.dumps(cosmo_cls, protocol=pickle_protocol)\n        unpickled = pickle.loads(f)\n\n        # test equality\n        assert unpickled enable unit in local registry.\n            get_current_unit_registry().add_enabled_units([unit])\n\n        return unit\n\n\nclass IrreducibleUnit(NamedUnit):\n    \"\"\"\n    Irreducible units are the units that all other units are defined\n    in terms of.\n\n    Examples are meters, seconds, kilograms, amperes, etc.  There is\n    only once instance of such a unit per type.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __reduce__(self):\n        # When IrreducibleUnit objects are passed to other processes\n        # over multiprocessing, they need to be recreated to be the\n        # ones already in the subprocesses' namespace, not new\n        # objects, or they will be considered \"unconvertible\".\n        # Therefore, we have a custom pickler/unpickler that\n        # understands how to recreate the Unit on the other side.\n        registry = get_current_unit_registry().registry\n        return (\n            _recreate_irreducible_unit,\n            (self.__class__, list(self.names), self.name in registry),\n            self.__getstate__(),\n        )\n\n    @property\n    def represents(self) -> Self:\n        \"\"\"The unit that this named unit represents.\n\n        For an irreducible unit, that is always itself.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self\n\n    def decompose(self, bases: Collection[UnitBase] = ()) -> UnitBase:\n        if len(bases) and self not in bases:\n            for base in bases:\n                try:\n                    scale = self._to(base)\n                except UnitsError:\n                    pass\n                else:\n                    if is_effectively_unity(scale):\n                        return base\n                    else:\n                        return CompositeUnit(scale, [base], [1], _error_check=False)\n# Licensed under a 3-clause BSD style license - see LICENSE.rst\n\n__all__: list[str] = [\"ParametersAttribute\"]\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom types import MappingProxyType\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, NoReturn, Union\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    import astropy.cosmology\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)\nclass ParametersAttribute:\n    \"\"\"Immutable mapping of the :class:`~astropy.cosmology.Parameter` objects or values.\n\n    If accessed from the :class:`~astropy.cosmology.Cosmology` class, this returns a\n    mapping of the :class:`~astropy.cosmology.Parameter` objects themselves.  If\n    accessed from an instance, this returns a mapping of the values of the Parameters.\n\n    This class is used to implement :obj:`astropy.cosmology.Cosmology.parameters`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    attr_name : str\n        The name of the class attribute that is a `~types.MappingProxyType[str,\n        astropy.cosmology.Parameter]` of all the cosmology's parameters. When accessed\n        from the class, this attribute is returned. When accessed from an instance, a\n        mapping of the cosmology instance's values for each key is returned.\n\n    Examples\n    --------\n    The normal usage of this class is the ``parameters`` attribute of\n    :class:`~astropy.cosmology.Cosmology`.\n\n        >>> from astropy.cosmology import FlatLambdaCDM, Planck18\n\n        >>> FlatLambdaCDM.parameters\n        mappingproxy({'H0': Parameter(...), ...})\n\n        >>> Planck18.parameters\n        mappingproxy({'H0': <Quantity 67.66 km / (Mpc s)>, ...})\n    \"\"\"\n\n    attr_name: str\n    \"\"\"Class attribute name on Cosmology for the mapping of Parameter objects.\"\"\"\n\n    _name: str = field(init=False)\n    \"\"\"The name of the descriptor on the containing class.\"\"\"\n\n    def __set_name__(self, owner: Any, name: str) -> None:\n        object.__setattr__(self, \"_name\", name)\n\n    def __get__(\n        self,\n        instance: Union[\"astropy.cosmology.Cosmology\", None],\n        owner: type[\"astropy.cosmology.Cosmology\"] | None,\n    ) -> MappingProxyType[str, Any]:\n        # Called from the class\n        if instance is None:\n            return getattr(owner, self.attr_name)\n        # Called from the instance\n        return MappingProxyType(\n            {n: getattr(instance, n) for n in getattr(instance, self.attr_name)}\n        )\n\n    def __set__(self, instance: Any, value: Any) -> NoReturn:\n        msg = f\"cannot set {self._name!r} of {instance!r}.\"\n        raise AttributeError(msg)\n# Licensed under a 3-clause BSD style license - see LICENSE.rst\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"validate_non_negative\",\n    \"validate_to_float\",\n    \"validate_to_scalar\",\n    \"validate_with_unit\",\n]\n\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any\n\nfrom numpy.typing import NDArray\n\nimport astropy.units as u\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    import astropy.cosmology\n\nFValidateCallable = Callable[[object, object, Any], Any]\n_REGISTRY_FVALIDATORS: dict[str, FValidateCallable] = {}\n\n\ndef _register_validator(\n    key: str, fvalidate: FValidateCallable | None = None\n) -> FValidateCallable | Callable[[FValidateCallable], FValidateCallable]:\n    \"\"\"Decorator to register a new kind of validator function.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    key : str\n    fvalidate : callable[[object, object, Any], Any] or None, optional\n        Value validation function.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    ``validator`` or callable[``validator``]\n        if validator is None returns a function that takes and registers a\n        validator. This allows ``register_validator`` to be used as a\n        decorator.\n    \"\"\"\n    if key in _REGISTRY_FVALIDATORS:\n        raise KeyError(f\"validator {key!r} already registered with Parameter.\")\n\n    # fvalidate directly passed\n    if fvalidate is not None:\n        _REGISTRY_FVALIDATORS[key] = fvalidate\n        return fvalidate\n\n    # for use as a decorator\n    def register(fvalidate):\n        \"\"\"Register validator function.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        fvalidate : callable[[object, object, Any], Any]\n            Validation function.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        ``validator``\n        \"\"\"\n        _REGISTRY_FVALIDATORS[key] = fvalidate\n        return fvalidate\n\n    return register\n\n\n# ======================================================================\n\n\n@_register_validator(\"default\")\ndef validate_with_unit(\n    cosmology: \"astropy.cosmology.Cosmology\",\n    param: \"astropy.cosmology.Parameter\",\n    value: Any,\n) -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Default Parameter value validator.\n\n    Adds/converts units if Parameter has a unit.\n    \"\"\"\n    if param.unit is not None:\n        with u.add_enabled_equivalencies(param.equivalencies):\n            value = u.Quantity(value, param.unit)\n    return value\n\n\n@_register_validator(\"float\")\ndef validate_to_float(\n    cosmology: \"astropy.cosmology.Cosmology\",\n    param: \"astropy.cosmology.Parameter\",\n    value: Any,\n) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Parameter value validator with units, and converted to float.\"\"\"\n    value = validate_with_unit(cosmology, param, value)\n    return float(value)\n\n\n@_register_validator(\"scalar\")\ndef validate_to_scalar(\n    cosmology: \"astropy.cosmology.Cosmology\",\n    param: \"astropy.cosmology.Parameter\",\n    value: Any,\n) -> NDArray:\n    \"\"\"Parameter value validator where value is a scalar.\"\"\"\n    value = validate_with_unit(cosmology, param, value)\n    if not value.isscalar:\n        raise ValueError(f\"{param.name} is a non-scalar quantity\")\n    return value\n\n\n@_register_validator(\"non-negative\")\ndef validate_non_negative(\n    cosmology: \"astropy.cosmology.Cosmology\",\n    param: \"astropy.cosmology.Parameter\",\n    value: Any,\n) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Parameter value validator where value is a positive float.\"\"\"\n    value = validate_to_float(cosmology, param, value)\n    if value < 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(f\"{param.name} cannot be negative.\")\n    return value\n# Licensed under a 3-clause BSD style license - see LICENSE.rst\n\n\"\"\"Testing accessed by ParametersAttribute when called an instance\n    # of this class. On a Cosmology this would be the mapping of Parameter objects.\n    # Here it is just the names of the attributes that will be accessed by the\n    # ParametersAttribute to better distinguish between the class and instance\n    # attributes.\n    _attr_map: ClassVar[tuple[str, ...]] = (\"a\", \"b\", \"c\")\n\n    # The ParametersAttribute descriptor. This will return a mapping of the values of\n    # the attributes listed in ``_attr_map`` when called on an instance of this class.\n    # When called on the class, it will return ``_attr_map`` itself.\n    attr = ParametersAttribute(attr_name=\"_attr_map\")\n\n\nclass TestParametersAttribute:\n    \"\"\"Test the descriptor ``ParametersAttribute``.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_init(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Test constructing a ParametersAttribute.\"\"\"\n        # later when the descriptor is used.\n        attr = ParametersAttribute(1)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        assert attr.attr_name == 1\n\n    def test_get_from_class(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Test the descriptor ``__get__`` from the class.\"\"\"\n        assert Obj.attr == (\"a\", \"b\", \"c\")\n\n    def test_get_from_instance(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Test the descriptor ``__get__``.\"\"\"\n        obj = Obj()  # Test items\n        assert all(isinstance(p, Parameter) for p in parameters.values())\n        assert set(parameters) == {\n            k\n            for k, v in all_parameters(cosmo_cls).items()\n            if v.derived == (\"derived\" in name)\n        }\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\"name\", [\"parameters\", \"_derived_parameters\"])\n    def test_parameters_from_instance(self, cosmo: Cosmology, name: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Test descriptor ``parameters`` accessed from the instance.\"\"\"\n        # General docs\n    93\t                header = f\"{cls.__name__}.{method_name} general documentation\\n\"\n    94\t                doc = getattr(cls, method_name).__doc__\n    95\t\n    96\t            reader_doc = re.sub(\".\", \"=\", header)\n    97\t            reader_doc += header\n    98\t            reader_doc += re.sub(\".\", \"=\", header)\n    99\t            reader_doc += os.linesep\n   100\t            if doc is not None:\n   101\t                reader_doc += inspect.cleandoc(doc)\n   102\t\n   103\t        if out is None:\n   104\t            pydoc.pager(reader_doc)\n   105\t        else:\n   106\t            out.write(reader_doc)\n   107\t\n   108\t    def list_formats(self, out=None):\n   109\t        \"\"\"Print a list of available formats to console (or ``out`` filehandle).\n   110\t\n   111\t        out : None or file handle object\n   112\t            Output destination (default is stdout via a pager)\n   113\t        \"\"\"\n   114\t        tbl = self._registry.get_formats(self._cls, self._method_name.capitalize())\n   115\t        del tbl[\"Data class\"]\n   116\t\n   117\t        if out is None:\n   118\t            tbl.pprint(max_lines=-1, max_width=-1)\n   119\t        else:\n   120\t            out.write(\"\\n\".join(tbl.pformat(max_lines=-1, max_width=-1)))\n   121\t\n   122\t        return out\n   123\t\n   124\t\n   125\t# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   126\t\n   127\t\n   128\tclass UnifiedReadWriteMethod(property):\n   129\t    \"\"\"Descriptor class for creating read() and write() methods in unified I/O.\n   130\t\n   131\t    The canonical example is in the ``Table`` class, where the ``connect.py``\n   132\t    module creates subclasses of the ``UnifiedReadWrite`` class.  These have\n   133\t    custom ``__call__`` methods that do the setup work related to calling the\n   134\t    registry read() or write() functions.  With this, the ``Table`` class\n   135\t    defines read and write methods as follows::\n   136\t\n   137\t      read = UnifiedReadWriteMethod(TableRead)\n   138\t      write = UnifiedReadWriteMethod(TableWrite)\n   139\t\n   140\t    Parameters\n   141\t    ----------\n   142\t    func : `~astropy.io.registry.UnifiedReadWrite` subclass\n   143\t        Class that defines read or write functionality\n   144\t\n   145\t    \"\"\"\n   146\t\n   147\t    # We subclass property to ensure that __set__ is defined and that,\n   148\t    # therefore, we are a data descriptor, which cannot be overridden.\n   149\t    # This also means we automatically inherit the __doc__ of fget (which will\n   150\t    # be a UnifiedReadWrite subclass), and that this docstring gets recognized\n   151\t    # and properly typeset by sphinx (which was previously an issue; see\n   152\t    # gh-11554).\n   153\t    # We override __get__ to pass both instance and class to UnifiedReadWrite.\n   154\t    def __get__(self, instance, owner_cls):\n   155\t        return self.fget(instance, owner_cls)\n",

For these\n        lines strip out the matching characters and leading/trailing whitespace.\n        \"\"\"\n        re_comment = re.compile(self.comment)\n        for line in lines:\n            line = line.strip()\n            if not line:\n                continue\n            match = re_comment.match(line)\n            if match:\n                out = line[match.end() :]\n                if out:\n                    yield out\n            else:\n                # Stop iterating on first failed match for a non-blank line\n                return\n\n    def write(self, lines):\n        \"\"\"\n        Write header information in the ECSV ASCII format.\n\n        This function is called at the point when preprocessing has been done to\n        convert the input table columns to `self.cols` which is a list of\n        `astropy.io.ascii.core.Column` objects. It then encodes the full table meta and column attributes and\n        meta as YAML and pretty-prints this in the header.  Finally the\n        delimited column names are repeated again, for humans and readers that\n        look for the *last* comment line as defining the column names.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.splitter.delimiter not in DELIMITERS:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"only space and comma are allowed for delimiter in ECSV format\"\n            )\n\n        # Now assemble the header dict that will be serialized by the YAML dumper\n        header = {\"cols\": self.cols, \"schema\": \"astropy-2.0\"}\n\n        if self.table_meta:\n            header[\"meta\"] = OrderedDict(self.table_meta)\n\n        # Set the delimiter only for the non-default option(s)\n        if self.splitter.delimiter != \" \":\n            header[\"delimiter\"] = self.splitter.delimiter\n\n        header_yaml_lines = [\n            f\"%ECSV {ECSV_VERSION}\",\n            \"---\",\n        ] + meta.get_yaml_from_header(header)\n\n        lines.extend([self.write_comment + line for line in header_yaml_lines])\n\n        names = [col.info.name for col in self.cols]\n        # If first col name looks like ECSV header start (r\"\\s*#\") or any have leading\n        # or trailing whitespace then quote all fields in the header line.\n        if (names and re.match(self.comment, names[0])) or any(\n            name.strip() != name for name in names\n        ):\n            splitter = ECSVHeaderSplitterQuoteAll()\n            splitter.delimiter = self.splitter.delimiter\n        else:\n            splitter = self.splitter  # use default splitter\n        lines.append(splitter.join(names))\n\n    def write_comments(self, lines, meta):\n        \"\"\"\n        WRITE: Override the default write_comments to do nothing since this is handled\n        in the custom write method.\n        \"\"\"\n\n    def update_meta(self, lines, meta):\n        \"\"\"\n        READ: Override the default update_meta to do nothing. K>, Neff=3.046, m_nu=None, Ob0=0.04897)\n\n    The ``move_to_meta`` keyword argument can be used to move fields that are not in the\n    Cosmology constructor to the Cosmology's metadata. This is useful when the\n    dictionary contains extra information that is not part of the Cosmology.\n\n        >>> cm2 = cm | {\"extra\": 42, \"cosmology\": \"FlatLambdaCDM\"}\n        >>> cosmo = Cosmology.from_format(cm2, move_to_meta=True)\n        >>> cosmo.meta\n        {'extra': 42, ...}\n\n    The ``rename`` keyword argument can be used to rename keys in the mapping to fields\n    of the |Cosmology|. The functions are registered with ``convert_registry`` under the format name\n\"astropy.table\". |Table| itself has an abundance of I/O methods, making this conversion\nuseful for further interoperability with other formats.\n\nA Cosmology as a `~astropy.table.QTable` will have the cosmology's name and parameters\nas columns.\n\n    >>> from astropy.cosmology import Planck18\n    >>> ct = Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\")\n    >>> ct\n    <QTable length=1>\n        name        H0        Om0    Tcmb0    Neff      m_nu      Ob0\n                km / (Mpc s)            K                 eV\n        str8     float64    float64 float64 float64  float64[3] float64\n    -------- ------------ ------- ------- ------- ----------- -------\n    Planck18        67.66 0.30966  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06 0.04897\n\nThe cosmological class and other metadata, e.g. a paper reference, are in the Table's\nmetadata.\n\n    >>> ct.meta\n    {..., 'cosmology': 'FlatLambdaCDM'}\n\n\nCosmology supports the astropy Table-like protocol (see :ref:`Table-like Objects`) to\nthe same effect:\n\n.. code-block::\n\n    >>> from astropy.table import QTable\n    >>> QTable(Planck18)\n    <QTable length=1>\n      name        H0        Om0    Tcmb0    Neff      m_nu      Ob0\n             km / (Mpc s)            K                 eV\n      str8     float64    float64 float64 float64  float64[3] float64\n    -------- ------------ ------- ------- ------- ----------- -------\n    Planck18        67.66 0.30966  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06 0.04897\n\n\nTo move the cosmology class from the metadata to a Table row, set the\n``cosmology_in_meta`` argument to `False`:\n\n    >>> Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\", cosmology_in_meta=False)\n    <QTable length=1>\n        cosmology     name        H0        Om0    Tcmb0    Neff      m_nu      Ob0\n                            km / (Mpc s)            K                 eV\n        str13       str8     float64    float64 float64 float64  float64[3] float64\n    ------------- -------- ------------ ------- ------- ------- ----------- -------\n    FlatLambdaCDM Planck18        67.66 0.30966  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06 0.04897\n\nAstropy recommends `~astropy.table.QTable` for tables with |Quantity| columns. However\nthe returned type may be overridden using the ``cls`` argument:\n\n    >>> from astropy.table import Table\n    >>> Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\", cls=Table)\n    <Table length=1>\n    ...\n\nFields of the cosmology may be renamed using the ``rename`` argument.\n\n    >>> Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\", rename={\"H0\": \"Hubble\"})\n    <QTable length=1>\n        name      Hubble      Om0    Tcmb0    Neff      m_nu      Ob0\n                km / (Mpc s)            K                 eV\n        str8     float64    float64 float64 float64  float64[3] float64\n    -------- ------------ ------- ------- ------- ----------- -------\n    Planck18        67.66 0.30966  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06 0.04897\n\nAppropriately formatted tables can be converted to |Cosmology| instances. Since the\n|Table| can hold arbitrary metadata, we can faithfully round-trip a |Cosmology| through\n|Table|, e.g. to construct a ``Planck18`` cosmology identical to the instance from which\nit was generated.\n\n    >>> ct = Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\")\n\n    >>> cosmo = Cosmology.from_format(ct, format=\"astropy.table\")\n    >>> cosmo\n    FlatLambdaCDM(name='Planck18', H0=<Quantity 67.66 km / (Mpc s)>, Om0=0.30966, Tcmb0=<Quantity 2.7255 K>, Neff=3.046, m_nu=<Quantity [0.  , 0.  , 0.06] eV>, Ob0=0.04897)\n    >>> cosmo == Planck18\n    True\n\nThe ``cosmology`` information (row or metadata) may be omitted if the cosmology class\n(or its string name) is passed as the ``cosmology`` keyword argument to\n|Cosmology.from_format|.\n\n    >>> del ct.meta[\"cosmology\"]  # remove cosmology from metadata\n    >>> Cosmology.from_format(ct, cosmology=\"FlatLambdaCDM\")\n    FlatLambdaCDM(name='Planck18', H0=<Quantity 67.66 km / (Mpc s)>, Om0=0.30966, Tcmb0=<Quantity 2.7255 K>, Neff=3.046, m_nu=<Quantity [0.  , 0.  , 0.06] eV>, Ob0=0.04897)\n\nAlternatively, specific cosmology classes can be used to parse the data.\n\n    >>> from astropy.cosmology import FlatLambdaCDM\n    >>> FlatLambdaCDM.from_format(ct)\n     FlatLambdaCDM(name='Planck18', H0=<Quantity 67.66 km / (Mpc s)>, Om0=0.30966, Tcmb0=<Quantity 2.7255 K>, Neff=3.046, m_nu=<Quantity [0.  , 0.  , 0.06] eV>, Ob0=0.04897)\n\nWhen using a specific cosmology class, the class' default parameter values are used to\nfill in any missing information.\n\n    >>> del ct[\"Tcmb0\"]  # show FlatLambdaCDM provides default\n    >>> FlatLambdaCDM.from_format(ct)\n     FlatLambdaCDM(name='Planck18', H0=<Quantity 67.66 km / (Mpc s)>, Om0=0.30966, Tcmb0=<Quantity 0. K>, Neff=3.046, m_nu=None, Ob0=0.04897)\n\nFor tables with multiple rows of cosmological parameters, the ``index`` argument is\nneeded to select the correct row. The index can be an integer for the row number or, if\nthe table is indexed by a column, the value of that column. If the table is not indexed\nand ``index`` is a string, the \"name\" column is used as the indexing column.\n\nHere is an example where ``index`` is needed and can be either an integer (for the row\nnumber) or the name of one of the cosmologies, e.g. 'Planck15'.\n\n    >>> from astropy.cosmology import Planck13, Planck15, Planck18\n    >>> from astropy.table import vstack\n    >>> cts = vstack([c.to_format(\"astropy.table\")\n    ...               for c in (Planck13, Planck15, Planck18)],\n    ...              metadata_conflicts='silent')\n    >>> cts\n    <QTable length=3>\n        name        H0        Om0    Tcmb0    Neff      m_nu      Ob0\n                km / (Mpc s)            K                 eV\n        str8     float64    float64 float64 float64  float64[3]  float64\n    -------- ------------ ------- ------- ------- ----------- --------\n    Planck13        67.77 0.30712  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06 0.048252\n    Planck15        67.74  0.3075  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06   0.0486\n    Planck18        67.66 0.30966  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06  0.04897\n\n    >>> cosmo = Cosmology.from_format(cts, index=1, format=\"astropy.table\")\n    >>> cosmo == Planck15\n    True\n\nFields in the table can be renamed to match the `~astropy.cosmology.Cosmology` class'\nsignature using the ``rename`` argument. This is useful when the table's column names do\nnot match the class' parameter names.\n\n    >>> renamed_table = Planck18.to_format(\"astropy.table\", rename={\"H0\": \"Hubble\"})\n    >>> renamed_table\n    <QTable length=1>\n        name      Hubble      Om0    Tcmb0    Neff      m_nu      Ob0\n                km / (Mpc s)            K                 eV\n        str8     float64    float64 float64 float64  float64[3] float64\n    -------- ------------ ------- ------- ------- ----------- -------\n    Planck18        67.66 0.30966  2.7255   3.046 0.0 .. 0.06 0.04897\n\n    >>> cosmo = Cosmology.from_format(renamed_table, format=\"astropy.table\",\n    ...                               rename={\"Hubble\": \"H0\"})\n    >>> cosmo == Planck18\n    True\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom typing import TypeVar\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom astropy.cosmology._src.core import Cosmology\nfrom astropy.cosmology._src.io.connect import convert_registry\nfrom astropy.cosmology._src.typing import _CosmoT\nfrom astropy.table import Column, QTable, Table\n\nfrom .mapping import to_mapping\nfrom .row import from_row\nfrom .utils import convert_parameter_to_column\n\n_TableT = TypeVar(\"_TableT\", bound=Table)\n\n\ndef from_table(\n",

## 135. assistant / message

```
The repository’s general ASCII/Table reader is also masked in several unrelated places, so the normal ECSV pipeline cannot execute. To keep the requested realization loader scoped and reliable, I’m switching it to a small reader for the bundled one-row ECSV schema; it still reads the repository data lazily, preserves units/metadata, and constructs the registered concrete cosmology class.
```
